Any recognised MD/MS/DNB broad specialty is eligible; Medical Group question paper is required. This does not open the other Medical Group destinations. |

### Superspecialty route details

- **DM / DrNB Medical Oncology** (Verified feeder). NEET-SS Medical Oncology Group; MCC SS counselling. Annexure A p116 explicitly lists MD/DNB Radiation Oncology for DM/DrNB Medical Oncology; reverse Annexure B confirms the same feeder. The Medical Oncology question paper tests Medical Oncology, not simply the parent MD syllabus. Radiotherapy wording in older degrees must be checked for accepted equivalence in the current application rules. INI-SS is a separate admissions system. Duration: DrNB: three years after eligible MD/MS/DNB. Confirm DM duration and all admitting-institute conditions in the current prospectus.. Sources: [NEET-SS 2025 Information Bulletin](https://nbe.edu.in/IB/NEET-SS%202025%20information%20bulletin.pdf) (pp 51, 55-56, 62, 64-69, 113-120; Annexures A and B); [NBEMS DrNB Super Specialty programme overview](https://natboard.edu.in/dnbsuper) (Overview, Admission and Counseling, Registration as DrNB Trainee)
- **DM / DrNB Medical Genetics** (Verified feeder). NEET-SS Medical Group; MCC SS counselling. Radiation Oncology is explicitly listed; Medical Genetics also accepts any recognised MD/MS/DNB broad specialty. This is a change to a different clinical field and requires its own aptitude and preparation. It does not open the other Medical Group destinations. Eligibility and securing a seat are separate. Duration: DrNB: three years after eligible MD/MS/DNB. Confirm DM duration and the admitting prospectus.. Sources: [NEET-SS 2025 Information Bulletin](https://nbe.edu.in/IB/NEET-SS%202025%20information%20bulletin.pdf) (pp 51, 55-56, 62, 64-69, 113-120; Annexures A and B); [NBEMS DrNB Super Specialty programme overview](https://natboard.edu.in/dnbsuper) (Overview, Admission and Counseling, Registration as DrNB Trainee)

### Fellowships and focused training

FNB and institutional awards are separate routes; verify current registration, recognition, privileges, intake, fees and stipend. This is an evidenced selection, not an exhaustive national fellowship register.

- **Advanced Gynaecologic Brachytherapy — Christian Medical College, Vellore**. Institutional post-doctoral fellowship. Entry: CMC's own application and selection; verify the current admissions notice. Eligibility: MD / DNB Radiation Oncology, explicitly listed. Duration: 1 year; prospectus lists intake 1. 2026-27 prospectus pp36,64. Includes CT/MRI-guided intracavitary, interstitial and hybrid brachytherapy, applicator insertion with intraoperative ultrasound guidance, planning, multidisciplinary care and research. This is an institutional fellowship, not a DM/DrNB or an assurance of current availability, stipend or faculty recognition. Sources: [CMC Vellore course prospectus 2026-27: Advanced Gynaecologic Brachytherapy fellowship](https://www.cmch-vellore.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/14/2026/06/course-prospectus-final-01-06-26.pdf) (PDF pp36 and64; filename version 01-06-26)
- **Advance Techniques in Radiation Oncology — Sanjay Gandhi Postgraduate Institute of Medical Sciences, Lucknow**. Post-Doctoral Certificate Course (PDCC). Entry: SGPGI 2026 institutional entrance/selection and counselling; separate from NEET-SS. Eligibility: MD / DNB Radiotherapy, explicitly listed; verify acceptance of your exact degree nomenclature. Duration: 1 year under the brochure's PDCC rule. Brochure pp4,7,16. Trainees also function as SRs with clinical, teaching, research and emergency duties. Non-sponsored emoluments follow first-year SR rules; sponsored trainees are paid by their sponsor. PDCC is separate from DM/DrNB, and no post-course pay premium is established. Sources: [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: Radiotherapy PDCC and senior residency](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,7,9-10,12,16)
- **Palliative Oncology — Sanjay Gandhi Postgraduate Institute of Medical Sciences, Lucknow**. Post-Doctoral Certificate Course (PDCC). Entry: SGPGI 2026 institutional entrance/selection and counselling; confirm the current seat notice. Eligibility: MD / DNB Radiotherapy, explicitly listed. Duration: 1 year under the brochure's PDCC rule. Brochure pp4,7,12,16. This is focused institutional training, not MD Palliative Medicine, an FNB award or another NEET-SS destination. Verify supervision, duties, sponsor status, intake and the exact certificate before applying. Sources: [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: Radiotherapy PDCC and senior residency](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,7,9-10,12,16)

### Other pathways

- **Deepen the parent specialty without changing to Medical Oncology**. Advanced external-beam planning, site-focused clinics and brachytherapy can form a coherent Radiation Oncology career. Ask specifically about actual contouring/plan review and supervised procedures. For stereotactic, adaptive or proton training, verify the named current programme, hands-on access, fee/stipend and subsequent privileges; no national fellowship inventory or pay premium for these labels is established here. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MD in Radiotherapy](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MD-Radiotherapy.pdf) (PDF pp1-6: preamble, competencies and practical skills; later curriculum sections); [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: Radiotherapy PDCC and senior residency](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,7,9-10,12,16); [CMC Vellore course prospectus 2026-27: Advanced Gynaecologic Brachytherapy fellowship](https://www.cmch-vellore.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/14/2026/06/course-prospectus-final-01-06-26.pdf) (PDF pp36 and64; filename version 01-06-26)
- **Medical Oncology senior residency is distinct from a Medical Oncology DM**. SGPGI's 2026 brochure p9 lists MD/DNB Medicine/Pediatrics/Radiotherapy/Medical Oncology for Medical Oncology SRHS. This is a time-limited service/training appointment, not a DM/DrNB award or proof that all employers accept the same qualification for consultant Medical Oncology posts. Sources: [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: Radiotherapy PDCC and senior residency](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,7,9-10,12,16)
- **Systemic therapy within documented training and clinical privileges**. The NMC MD Radiotherapy curriculum explicitly includes cytotoxic drugs, combined treatment and oncology emergencies. Do not erase that training, but do not infer unrestricted permission for every systemic regimen or equivalence to DM Medical Oncology. Check current registration rules, actual supervised competence, credentialing, inpatient support and employer privileges. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MD in Radiotherapy](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MD-Radiotherapy.pdf) (PDF pp1-6: preamble, competencies and practical skills; later curriculum sections); [NEET-SS 2025 Information Bulletin](https://nbe.edu.in/IB/NEET-SS%202025%20information%20bulletin.pdf) (pp 51, 55-56, 62, 64-69, 113-120; Annexures A and B)
- **Research / PhD and international training**. The curriculum supports research, trials and academic development; CMC's focused fellowship includes research and dosimetry. A PhD or overseas fellowship requires its own funded programme and admissions evidence. Observation, training and a licence to practise abroad are separate; no automatic international licence or salary equivalence is inferred. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MD in Radiotherapy](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MD-Radiotherapy.pdf) (PDF pp1-6: preamble, competencies and practical skills; later curriculum sections); [CMC Vellore course prospectus 2026-27: Advanced Gynaecologic Brachytherapy fellowship](https://www.cmch-vellore.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/14/2026/06/course-prospectus-final-01-06-26.pdf) (PDF pp36 and64; filename version 01-06-26)

### Competition, differentiation and shortlisting

My shortlisting judgement: this is a plausible choice for someone who likes cancer medicine, imaging-based precision, treatment planning and long-term patient care, and who accepts that employment is tied closely to functioning treatment centres. It is less suited to someone whose main aim is a low-capital independent clinic in any chosen town. Assess the number of funded consultant posts and available case responsibility in places you would live; machine installations, cancer incidence and seat growth alone cannot tell you whether graduates face a surplus or shortage.

**Evidence limits:** This source set contains curricula, admissions rules, official notices and a small convenience sample of adverts. It contains no representative Radiation Oncology unemployment survey or realised salary distribution by city, experience or sector. Undated portal adverts cannot establish a 2025-to-2026 pay trend. Corporate-chain pay and fellowship/DM income increments remain unverified.

- Build a city-specific list of operational centres, their consultant teams and actual hiring over the last 12-24 months. Speak with recent alumni about time to first job, fixed pay and geographic moves.
- Ask for trainee-level numbers: supervised contours and plan approvals, disease-site mix, IMRT/VMAT and image-guidance exposure, and brachytherapy insertions personally performed. Equipment advertised on a website is not your case log.
- Check medical-physics staffing, treatment-planning access, machine uptime and service cover. Ask what happens to patients, training and pay during downtime.
- For a new centre, confirm that funding, equipment, commissioning, staffing, approvals and a realistic referral pathway exist. A promised future machine is not a present clinical service.
- Assess surgical/medical oncology collaboration, pathology/imaging turnaround, tumour boards, emergency cover, toxicity support and continuity clinics.
- Compare fixed salary, incentives and targets, planning/patient load, weekend cover, leave, indemnity and restrictive contract clauses in writing. Treatment revenue is not personal earnings.
- Investigate the psychological workload of cancer care, recurrence, death and difficult family conversations; do not assume all work is elective or free of emergencies.
- Choose Radiation Oncology for the parent specialty you would be willing to practise. Treat Medical Oncology as a possible further route, not a guaranteed rescue plan.
- Medical Oncology and Medical Genetics are two destination subjects with four award-specific options in this NEET-SS baseline. The seats accept other eligible feeders too and are not reserved for Radiation Oncology graduates.
- For central, state, deemed and private colleges, compare the actual department rather than ownership alone: case mix, faculty continuity, physics support, supervised planning, procedures, research and alumni employment.
- A busy centre can still provide poor hands-on training if trainees only do paperwork; ask current residents to show how cases and procedures are allocated.
- My opinion: prioritise a reliably functioning service with strong supervision and documented clinical responsibility over a prestigious label with uncertain access. We have not independently ranked Radiation Oncology departments.
- Compare one focused year of training with entering a supported job; a certificate's benefit depends on skills, vacancies and the opportunity cost of delayed consultant income.

Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MD in Radiotherapy](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MD-Radiotherapy.pdf) (PDF pp1-6: preamble, competencies and practical skills; later curriculum sections); [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: Radiotherapy PDCC and senior residency](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,7,9-10,12,16); [CMC Vellore course prospectus 2026-27: Advanced Gynaecologic Brachytherapy fellowship](https://www.cmch-vellore.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/14/2026/06/course-prospectus-final-01-06-26.pdf) (PDF pp36 and64; filename version 01-06-26); [AIIMS Raebareli ad-hoc Senior Resident recruitment, AIIMS/RBL/REC/SR/2026/532](https://aiimsrbl.edu.in/public/recruitments%5Cdownload%5C124) (pp1-2 departments and vacancies; p3 qualification, pay and appointment conditions; p4 excluded regular-service benefits); [AIIMS Raebareli faculty recruitment, AIIMS/RBL/REC/FACULTY/2026/467](https://aiimsrbl.edu.in/public/recruitments%5Cdownload%5C118) (pp2-6 qualifications/experience; p7 pay tables; pp8-9 department/rank vacancies; p15 contractual conditions); [Consultant Radiation Oncologist advert, Jaipur, Rajasthan (PlacementIndia job 1282673)](https://www.placementindia.com/job-detail/radiation-oncologist-in-recmasters-at-jaipur-1282673.htm) (Job title, salary/experience header, Job Description and qualification fields)

**Open evidence gaps**

- No representative India-wide salary distribution, measured national saturation rate or guaranteed earnings ladder was established.
- No verified named corporate-chain salary offer by Tier 1/Tier 2 and career stage was obtained. Recruiters' unnamed hospital clients remain Private, not Corporate chain.
- The private salary adverts below did not expose reliable publication dates in the consulted copies. They are retrieval-dated observations, not confirmed 2026 offers; some qualification and experience fields are incomplete or inconsistent.
- No matched before/after salary evidence establishes a fellowship premium or earnings after DM/DrNB Medical Oncology or Medical Genetics specifically for a Radiation Oncology graduate.
- Seat counts are the existing initial MCC NEET-SS 2025 baseline published 11 March 2026; later changes, INI-SS and a NEET-SS 2026 matrix are not included.
- The three named focused programmes are verified examples, not an exhaustive list, an FNB eligibility claim or current vacancy counts.
- Exact current degree equivalence, faculty-recognition rules, licence requirements and individual clinical privileges must be checked with the competent authority and employer.

### Editorial career planning

My shortlisting opinion: consider Radiation Oncology if you enjoy cancer medicine, treatment planning, imaging anatomy and sustained patient relationships, and are willing to work where treatment infrastructure and a funded role exist. I would not choose it solely as a presumed route to Medical Oncology or assume a new machine guarantees a job. A strong, functioning department with hands-on supervision would weigh more heavily for me than central/state/deemed/private ownership alone.

- **After MD/DNB:** Compare SR/supported consultant roles and assess readiness for independent case management. Public SR examples give INR 67,700 basic/month plus applicable allowances. Portal adverts have different annual figures, qualifications and evidence quality; they are not a market average.
- **Focused fellowship / PDCC:** Three verified examples cover advanced techniques, palliative oncology and gynaecologic brachytherapy. Compare real case logs, supervision, certificate, stipend/fees, sponsor rules and one year's opportunity cost. No matched salary premium is established.
- **DM/DrNB training:** Medical Oncology and Medical Genetics are eligible destinations in NEET-SS 2025, with four award options. Select the specialty and required exam group deliberately; qualifying eligibility does not reserve a seat or guarantee future earnings.
- **After SS / established clinician:** A Medical Oncology or Genetics career requires the appropriate training, role and privileges. Senior Radiation Oncology practice/academia is also a distinct path. The observed Associate Professor and unnamed head-role adverts do not supply a universal senior-consultant salary band.

- Demonstrated clinical judgement, contouring/plan evaluation, toxicity management and supervised procedural competence within your privileges.
- A real funded post, predictable planning and patient workload, and functioning physics/equipment support in a city you can accept.
- Site-focused or brachytherapy skills that meet a specific employer's need. A certificate, expensive machine or further DM does not establish an automatic salary uplift.
- Written fixed pay and incentive rules, on-call responsibilities and patient allocation; hospital treatment receipts are not personal earnings.
- Ask recent graduates how long they took to find a funded job, in which city and at what fixed pay; speak to more than one department's alumni.
- Compare operational equipment, supervised contours, plan-review frequency, brachytherapy procedures personally performed and emergency support, not just the advertised technology list.
- Check current exact feeder equivalence, institute rules, faculty recognition and clinical privileges for your degree and any fellowship.
- Named corporate-chain salary data, a representative Tier 1/Tier 2 comparison and post-fellowship/SS earnings remain gaps. Undated adverts do not establish a pay trend.

## General Surgery

General Surgery is a clinical and operative career involving assessment, consent, preoperative preparation, surgery, perioperative care, emergencies and follow-up. MS/DNB can lead to parent-specialty practice, academic or hospital employment, focused training, or a later MCh/DrNB/DM route where specifically eligible. A further superspecialty is not automatically required for every General Surgery job. Your actual independent operating experience, complication management, referral network and hospital support should guide the first job; a prestigious degree or a short certificate is not a substitute for supervised competence.

### Immediately after MD/MS

- **Senior resident / supported surgical clinician** — Government, teaching or other supervised surgical hospital. Consolidate operative decision-making, graded responsibility, emergency care and postoperative management. The September 2026 AIIMS Raebareli notice lists seven General Surgery SR vacancies. SGPGI accepts General Surgery for specified surgical SRHS departments; working in one of those departments does not award an MCh/DrNB. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MS in General Surgery](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MS-Surgery.pdf) (PDF pp1-2 learning objectives; competencies, operative skills and assessment sections); [AIIMS Raebareli ad-hoc Senior Resident recruitment, AIIMS/RBL/REC/SR/2026/532](https://aiimsrbl.edu.in/public/recruitments%5Cdownload%5C124) (pp1-2 departments and vacancies; p3 qualification, pay and appointment conditions; p4 excluded regular-service benefits); [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: surgery PDCC and SRHS routes](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,6-10,12,16; PDCC qualifications, SRHS department rows, duration and pay)
- **General surgeon / consultant general and laparoscopic surgeon** — Private, trust, district or corporate hospital with suitable services and privileges. The parent degree can support a General Surgery career within training, registration and privileges. Advertised consultant roles may expect independent laparoscopy, emergency laparotomy and complication management. Match the job to proven competence and senior/anaesthesia/ICU support rather than treating every 'fresher' advert as a safe first independent role. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MS in General Surgery](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MS-Surgery.pdf) (PDF pp1-2 learning objectives; competencies, operative skills and assessment sections); [Consultant General Surgeon advert, Satna, Madhya Pradesh (PlacementIndia 1462771)](https://www.placementindia.com/job-detail/general-surgeon-in-pace-consultants-at-satna-1462771.htm) (Job title, annual salary header, experience/education fields, Job Description and interview location where applicable); [General Surgeon advert, Rajahmundry (Rajamahendravaram), Andhra Pradesh (PlacementIndia 1453299)](https://www.placementindia.com/job-detail/general-surgeon-in-medico-hr-services-at-rajahmundry-1453299.htm) (Job title, annual salary header, experience/education fields, Job Description and interview location where applicable)
- **Academic surgeon** — Medical college or university surgical department. Combine operating, patient care, teaching, audit and research. Current regulations and the exact advertisement govern faculty eligibility. AIIMS Raebareli's August 2026 matrix has General Surgery Professor and Associate Professor vacancies, with rank-specific experience requirements; these pay observations are not first-job salaries. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MS in General Surgery](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MS-Surgery.pdf) (PDF pp1-2 learning objectives; competencies, operative skills and assessment sections); [AIIMS Raebareli faculty recruitment, AIIMS/RBL/REC/FACULTY/2026/467](https://aiimsrbl.edu.in/public/recruitments%5Cdownload%5C118) (pp2-6 qualifications/experience; p7 pay tables; pp8-9 department/rank vacancies; p15 contractual conditions)
- **Focused surgical skill development** — Accredited FNB centre or verified institutional programme. Develop a chosen area such as minimal-access, colorectal, abdominal-wall, breast/endocrine or head/neck surgery. Some FNBs require extra experience or a prior superspecialty. Compare actual trainee case logs, graded operating, complication rescue, credential and cost before selecting a programme. Sources: [FET 2025 information bulletin: General Surgery fellowship feeders](https://www.nbe.edu.in/IB/FET%202025%20information%20bulletin%2012.01.2026_v2.pdf) (PDF pp25,30-33,130-132; Annexure B, Indian-candidate table); [CMC Vellore 2026-27 prospectus: General Surgery focused fellowships](https://www.cmch-vellore.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/14/2026/06/course-prospectus-final-01-06-26.pdf) (PDF pp33-36 eligibility/duration; pp42,64-67 programme descriptions and hands-on training); [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: surgery PDCC and SRHS routes](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,6-10,12,16; PDCC qualifications, SRHS department rows, duration and pay)
- **Emergency / trauma surgical career** — Hospital with funded acute-care work, anaesthesia, blood bank, imaging and critical-care backup. The curriculum includes urgent referral, trauma and resuscitation. FNB Trauma & Acute Care Surgery and SGPGI's Trauma/Resuscitative Emergency PDCCs provide separately evidenced focused routes. Their existence does not establish universal privileges or NEET-SS Critical Care eligibility. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MS in General Surgery](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MS-Surgery.pdf) (PDF pp1-2 learning objectives; competencies, operative skills and assessment sections); [FET 2025 information bulletin: General Surgery fellowship feeders](https://www.nbe.edu.in/IB/FET%202025%20information%20bulletin%2012.01.2026_v2.pdf) (PDF pp25,30-33,130-132; Annexure B, Indian-candidate table); [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: surgery PDCC and SRHS routes](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,6-10,12,16; PDCC qualifications, SRHS department rows, duration and pay)
- **Hospital-linked practice and later service leadership** — A supported surgical service, with ownership or independent contracts considered separately. A general surgeon's clinic still needs an appropriate operative/referral setting. Evaluate patient acquisition, theatre access, anaesthesia, complications and continuity before planning ownership. Clinical audit, consent, record keeping and continuing development are curriculum obligations; business revenue and procedure charges do not equal take-home earnings. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MS in General Surgery](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MS-Surgery.pdf) (PDF pp1-2 learning objectives; competencies, operative skills and assessment sections)

### Superspecialty route details

- **MCh / DrNB Plastic and Reconstructive Surgery** (Verified feeder). NEET-SS 2025 Surgical Group; MCC SS counselling. MS/DNB General Surgery is explicitly eligible in Annexure A p114 and the corresponding reverse Annexure B pp117-120. Reconstructive work, burns and later focused practice are distinct from simply adding cosmetic procedures to a General Surgery clinic. Duration: DrNB: three years after the eligible postgraduate feeder. For MCh/DM, confirm duration and all current conditions in the admitting prospectus.. Sources: [NEET-SS 2025 Information Bulletin](https://nbe.edu.in/IB/NEET-SS%202025%20information%20bulletin.pdf) (pp 51, 55-56, 62, 64-69, 113-120; Annexures A and B); [NBEMS DrNB Super Specialty programme overview](https://natboard.edu.in/dnbsuper) (Overview, Admission and Counseling, Registration as DrNB Trainee)
- **MCh / DrNB Surgical Gastroenterology** (Verified feeder). NEET-SS 2025 Surgical Group; MCC SS counselling. MS/DNB General Surgery is explicitly eligible in Annexure A p114 and the corresponding reverse Annexure B pp117-120. A route to advanced GI surgery; also the specified prerequisite for FNB Liver Transplantation. Training should include supervised operating and perioperative/complication management. Duration: DrNB: three years after the eligible postgraduate feeder. For MCh/DM, confirm duration and all current conditions in the admitting prospectus.. Sources: [NEET-SS 2025 Information Bulletin](https://nbe.edu.in/IB/NEET-SS%202025%20information%20bulletin.pdf) (pp 51, 55-56, 62, 64-69, 113-120; Annexures A and B); [NBEMS DrNB Super Specialty programme overview](https://natboard.edu.in/dnbsuper) (Overview, Admission and Counseling, Registration as DrNB Trainee)
- **MCh Hepato Pancreato Biliary Surgery** (Verified feeder). NEET-SS 2025 Surgical Group; MCC SS counselling. MS/DNB General Surgery is explicitly eligible in Annexure A p114 and the corresponding reverse Annexure B pp117-120. Dedicated HPB surgery is a separate eligible destination. An HPB fellowship and an MCh have different credentials and selection routes. Duration: Confirm duration and all current conditions in the admitting institute's MCh/DM prospectus; DrNB duration is not used as proof for this different award.. Sources: [NEET-SS 2025 Information Bulletin](https://nbe.edu.in/IB/NEET-SS%202025%20information%20bulletin.pdf) (pp 51, 55-56, 62, 64-69, 113-120; Annexures A and B)
- **MCh / DrNB Urology** (Verified feeder). NEET-SS 2025 Surgical Group; MCC SS counselling. MS/DNB General Surgery is explicitly eligible in Annexure A p114 and the corresponding reverse Annexure B pp117-120. A separate surgical specialty, with subsequent FNB Minimal Access Urology and eligible transplant/paediatric-urology options under their own feeder rules. Duration: DrNB: three years after the eligible postgraduate feeder. For MCh/DM, confirm duration and all current conditions in the admitting prospectus.. - **MCh / DrNB Vascular Surgery** (Verified feeder). NEET-SS 2025 Surgical Group; MCC SS counselling. MS/DNB General Surgery is explicitly eligible in Annexure A p114 and the corresponding reverse Annexure B pp117-120. Requires appropriate operative/endovascular exposure, imaging and hospital support. Matrix DrNB Peripheral Vascular Surgery is mapped to this destination. Duration: DrNB: three years after the eligible postgraduate feeder. For MCh/DM, confirm duration and all current conditions in the admitting prospectus.. ### Fellowships and focused training

FNB and institutional awards are separate routes; verify current registration, recognition, privileges, intake, fees and stipend. This is an evidenced selection, not an exhaustive national fellowship register.

- **Minimal Access Surgery — NBEMS-accredited institution; verify the actual FET counselling matrix**. Fellowship National Board (FNB). Entry: FET 2025 and NBEMS fellowship counselling; separate from NEET-SS. Eligibility: MS/DNB General Surgery. Duration: 2 years under the official FNB programme overview. A focused route for supervised laparoscopic/minimal-access competence; the degree alone is not evidence of adequate independent operating. FNB, MCh/DM/DrNB and institutional certificates are separate credentials. Verify current course-specific recognition, centre accreditation, clinical privileges and teaching appointment eligibility. Accredited seats are not necessarily offered counselling seats. Sources: [FET 2025 information bulletin: General Surgery fellowship feeders](https://www.nbe.edu.in/IB/FET%202025%20information%20bulletin%2012.01.2026_v2.pdf) (PDF pp25,30-33,130-132; Annexure B, Indian-candidate table); [NBEMS Fellowship programme overview: duration and entry](https://natboard.edu.in/dnbfellow) (Fellowship: programme overview and admission sections)
- **Colorectal Surgery — NBEMS-accredited institution; verify the actual FET counselling matrix**. Fellowship National Board (FNB). Entry: FET 2025 and NBEMS fellowship counselling; separate from NEET-SS. Eligibility: MS/DNB General Surgery. Duration: 2 years under the official FNB programme overview. Separate FNB credential and selection; verify supervised operating, colorectal case mix and complication support. FNB, MCh/DM/DrNB and institutional certificates are separate credentials. Verify current course-specific recognition, centre accreditation, clinical privileges and teaching appointment eligibility. Accredited seats are not necessarily offered counselling seats. Sources: [FET 2025 information bulletin: General Surgery fellowship feeders](https://www.nbe.edu.in/IB/FET%202025%20information%20bulletin%2012.01.2026_v2.pdf) (PDF pp25,30-33,130-132; Annexure B, Indian-candidate table); [NBEMS Fellowship programme overview: duration and entry](https://natboard.edu.in/dnbfellow) (Fellowship: programme overview and admission sections)
- **Hand & Micro Surgery — NBEMS-accredited institution; verify the actual FET counselling matrix**. Fellowship National Board (FNB). Entry: FET 2025 and NBEMS fellowship counselling; separate from NEET-SS. Eligibility: MS/DNB General Surgery is an explicitly permitted feeder, alongside Orthopaedics and Plastic/Reconstructive Surgery. Duration: 2 years under the official FNB programme overview. General Surgery eligibility does not guarantee equal pre-entry microsurgical exposure; verify the required skills and supervision. FNB, MCh/DM/DrNB and institutional certificates are separate credentials. Verify current course-specific recognition, centre accreditation, clinical privileges and teaching appointment eligibility. Accredited seats are not necessarily offered counselling seats. Sources: [FET 2025 information bulletin: General Surgery fellowship feeders](https://www.nbe.edu.in/IB/FET%202025%20information%20bulletin%2012.01.2026_v2.pdf) (PDF pp25,30-33,130-132; Annexure B, Indian-candidate table); [NBEMS Fellowship programme overview: duration and entry](https://natboard.edu.in/dnbfellow) (Fellowship: programme overview and admission sections)

Entry: FET 2025 and NBEMS fellowship counselling; separate from NEET-SS. Eligibility: MS/DNB General Surgery is an explicitly permitted feeder; other named ENT/Surgical Oncology/Head and Neck qualifications are separately listed. Duration: 2 years under the official FNB programme overview. Distinct from MCh Head and Neck Surgery and from an institutional Head/Neck fellowship. FNB, MCh/DM/DrNB and institutional certificates are separate credentials. Verify current course-specific recognition, centre accreditation, clinical privileges and teaching appointment eligibility. Accredited seats are not necessarily offered counselling seats. Entry: FET 2025 and NBEMS fellowship counselling; separate from NEET-SS. Eligibility: MS/DNB General Surgery; MS Traumatology and Surgery also listed. Duration: 2 years under the official FNB programme overview. A focused FNB route; emergency duties, trauma operating, ICU backup and supervision require review. FNB, MCh/DM/DrNB and institutional certificates are separate credentials. Verify current course-specific recognition, centre accreditation, clinical privileges and teaching appointment eligibility. Accredited seats are not necessarily offered counselling seats. Entry: FET 2025 and NBEMS fellowship counselling; separate from NEET-SS. Eligibility: MS/DNB General Surgery PLUS at least three years' post-PG experience in General Surgery / Minimal Access Surgery. Duration: 2 years under the official FNB programme overview. Not a fresh-MS entry solely on the degree. Verify documentary evidence of the experience and the applicable date cutoff before applying. FNB, MCh/DM/DrNB and institutional certificates are separate credentials. Verify current course-specific recognition, centre accreditation, clinical privileges and teaching appointment eligibility. Accredited seats are not necessarily offered counselling seats. - **Renal Transplant — NBEMS-accredited institution; verify the actual FET counselling matrix**. Fellowship National Board (FNB). Entry: FET 2025 and NBEMS fellowship counselling; separate from NEET-SS. Eligibility: MCh/DNB/DrNB Urology OR MS/DNB General Surgery with three years' experience in a renal-transplant centre approved by the Renal Transplant Specialty Board for that experience. Duration: 2 years under the official FNB programme overview. Conditional post-MS route: ordinary surgical experience or an unapproved centre does not satisfy the stated requirement. FNB, MCh/DM/DrNB and institutional certificates are separate credentials. Verify current course-specific recognition, centre accreditation, clinical privileges and teaching appointment eligibility. Accredited seats are not necessarily offered counselling seats. Duration: 2 years under the official FNB programme overview. Sequence: eligible post-MS Surgical Gastroenterology superspecialty, then this FET route. Do not count this as immediately available after MS. FNB, MCh/DM/DrNB and institutional certificates are separate credentials. Verify current course-specific recognition, centre accreditation, clinical privileges and teaching appointment eligibility. Accredited seats are not necessarily offered counselling seats. Duration: 2 years under the official FNB programme overview. A post-SS FNB route with its own admission/credential requirements, not a substitute for the Urology qualification. ### Other pathways

- **Build a durable General Surgery practice without mandatory MCh**. General Surgery itself has defined clinical and surgical competencies. A supported job plus targeted skill consolidation can be a coherent route. Whether it succeeds in your city depends on patient/referral access, case allocation, hospital support and demonstrated competence; neither guaranteed independent success nor compulsory superspecialisation is established by these sources. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MS in General Surgery](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MS-Surgery.pdf) (PDF pp1-2 learning objectives; competencies, operative skills and assessment sections); [General Surgeon advert, Rajahmundry (Rajamahendravaram), Andhra Pradesh (PlacementIndia 1453299)](https://www.placementindia.com/job-detail/general-surgeon-in-medico-hr-services-at-rajahmundry-1453299.htm) (Job title, annual salary header, experience/education fields, Job Description and interview location where applicable); [Consultant General Surgeon advert, Satna, Madhya Pradesh (PlacementIndia 1462771)](https://www.placementindia.com/job-detail/general-surgeon-in-pace-consultants-at-satna-1462771.htm) (Job title, annual salary header, experience/education fields, Job Description and interview location where applicable)
- **Three exam groups for the complete eligible NEET-SS set**. Twelve destination subjects use Surgical Group. Head and Neck Surgery uses ENT Group; Medical Genetics uses Medical Group. Select and prepare for the appropriate paper(s). A single Surgical Group score does not open all 14 listed subjects, and seats are shared with other eligible feeders where applicable. Sources: [NEET-SS 2025 Information Bulletin](https://nbe.edu.in/IB/NEET-SS%202025%20information%20bulletin.pdf) (pp 51, 55-56, 62, 64-69, 113-120; Annexures A and B)
- **Higher-stage transplant and focused urology fellowships**. FNB Liver Transplantation needs MCh/DrNB GI Surgery; Minimal Access Urology needs Urology SS; Paediatric Urology needs Urology or Paediatric Surgery SS. FNB Renal Transplant separately permits General Surgery with three years at an approved renal-transplant centre. Degree labels and documented experience must match the FET table. Sources: [FET 2025 information bulletin: General Surgery fellowship feeders](https://www.nbe.edu.in/IB/FET%202025%20information%20bulletin%2012.01.2026_v2.pdf) (PDF pp25,30-33,130-132; Annexure B, Indian-candidate table)
- **SRHS, research and patient-safety work**. SGPGI lists General Surgery feeders for several surgical SRHS services and Resuscitative Emergency Medicine. Its Patient Safety including Infection Control PDCC admits any broad specialty under the stated rule. These can supply service, training or quality-development experience, but do not convert the qualification into the host department's superspecialty degree. Sources: [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: surgery PDCC and SRHS routes](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,6-10,12,16; PDCC qualifications, SRHS department rows, duration and pay); [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MS in General Surgery](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MS-Surgery.pdf) (PDF pp1-2 learning objectives; competencies, operative skills and assessment sections)
- **International training and professional-college qualifications**. Overseas exams, fellowships, registration and independent specialist recognition are separate requirements. Verify the destination regulator, training accreditation, licence and funded post. This research does not establish an automatic overseas specialist licence, MRCS/FRCS equivalence to an Indian MCh, or an international salary route. Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MS in General Surgery](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MS-Surgery.pdf) (PDF pp1-2 learning objectives; competencies, operative skills and assessment sections)

### Salary and stipend observations

- **Associate Professor (regular/deputation pay scale), AIIMS Raebareli, Raebareli, Uttar Pradesh (Other / smaller city)**: INR 138,300–209,200 per month; Pay-matrix basic. Stage: Postgraduate academic career: Associate Professor. Qualification: Recognised MD/MS or accepted equivalent in the concerned broad specialty; relevant DM/MCh/equivalent additionally required for superspecialty posts.. Experience: Six years of recognised teaching/research after the qualifying MD/MS/equivalent for broad specialties; three years after a recognised three-year DM/MCh for superspecialty appointments, with other historical routes separately specified.. Level13-A1+ monthly BASIC scale; applicable allowances and NPA are separate and have not been calculated. Scale maximum is a progression endpoint, not an advertised fresh-graduate package or guaranteed offer. Mapped specialties have a nonzero vacancy at this rank in the dated advertisement; category, selection and full eligibility still apply. Sources: [AIIMS Raebareli faculty recruitment, AIIMS/RBL/REC/FACULTY/2026/467](https://aiimsrbl.edu.in/public/recruitments%5Cdownload%5C118) (pp2-6 qualifications/experience; p7 pay tables; pp8-9 department/rank vacancies; p15 contractual conditions)
- **Associate Professor (contractual appointment), AIIMS Raebareli, Raebareli, Uttar Pradesh (Other / smaller city)**: INR 188,000–188,000 per month; Consolidated remuneration. Stage: Postgraduate academic career: Associate Professor. Qualification: Concerned postgraduate specialty and full faculty eligibility required.. Experience: Six years of recognised teaching/research after the qualifying MD/MS/equivalent for broad specialties; three years after a recognised three-year DM/MCh for superspecialty appointments, with other historical routes separately specified.. Advertised monthly lump-sum contractual remuneration; do not add regular-pay allowances by assumption. Retired faculty consultant designations/accommodation deductions have separate conditions; these are not used as a general private-consultant salary. Experience: Completed eligible postgraduate feeder qualification; this payment is during further training.. 2025 admission guidelines monthly stipend, corroborated by the signed 7 September 2026 FY2026-27 disclosure at the same amounts; not post-training consultant income or evidence of a stipend increase. The website link label says FY2025-26; the scanned linked document explicitly says FY2026-2027. Its date/heading govern this source description. Parent mapping is limited to actual TMC DM/MCh destinations in the initial matrix and eligible feeders; it does not imply every course is open to every mapped parent. ### Competition, differentiation and shortlisting

My shortlisting judgement: General Surgery preserves many later training choices and can also be a long-term parent-specialty career, but its value depends heavily on real operating exposure, judgement and access to a supported surgical service. I would favour it for someone who actively wants operative work, perioperative responsibility and emergencies, and can tolerate a variable early transition to independent practice. A high number of SS seats is an option set, not a guarantee of a job or a reason that every graduate must superspecialise. Competition must be assessed by city, hospital and case/referral access.

**Evidence limits:** The evidence is curricula, official eligibility/matrices, recruitment notices and a small, unverified convenience sample of adverts. No representative national General Surgery unemployment rate, realised earnings distribution or causal city-tier pay comparison was established. Portal adverts frequently disagree on salary, work hours, qualification or location. National 'saturated' or 'shortage' labels and a fixed fellowship/MCh income premium cannot be inferred.

- Ask residents for individual primary-operator and assisted case logs, not total department volume: hernia, appendicectomy, gallbladder, bowel, breast/thyroid and emergency work as applicable to the curriculum and local privileges.
- Check how cases are divided among MS/DNB trainees, senior residents, fellows and MCh trainees; observe whether you perform supervised operations or mostly observe and complete ward work.
- Assess open and laparoscopic exposure, conversion/complication rescue, postoperative care and supervision. Robotic equipment or a high case count does not establish trainee console/operating access.
- Compare anaesthesia, blood bank, ICU/HDU, imaging, endoscopy, nursing, referral arrangements and out-of-hours senior cover at both the training institution and proposed employer.
- Ask recent alumni about the first supported role, time until independent operating, moves between cities and actual fixed pay. Do not substitute a recruiter budget for these outcomes.
- For every offer, separate fixed salary, retainer, per-case fees and collections incentives. Verify OP/OT allocation, patient numbers, call roster, emergency duties, leave, indemnity and complication responsibility in writing.
- For focused practice, investigate local case/referral demand and how hospitals credential the exact fellowship. A certificate does not automatically change faculty eligibility or authorise every procedure.
- Compare further training's fees, stipend, bond and delayed earnings with entering a supported General Surgery job. Pick an MCh/FNB for the work and training, not solely a headline package.
- Consider physical stamina, disrupted sleep, exposure to emergencies and the emotional responsibility for surgical complications; shortlisting for 'lifestyle' requires checking the actual roster.
- Prioritise liking operative work and perioperative care. I would not choose General Surgery only as an indirect route to one highly competitive superspecialty.
- The strongest department for you may be central, state, deemed or private: compare supervised operating responsibility, faculty continuity, emergency mix, infrastructure, costs and graduate outcomes. This research does not independently rank surgical departments.
- A broad MS/DNB job and an MCh/DrNB career are different valid pathways. Training quality and funded demand determine how useful either is in your chosen geography.
- Distinguish direct post-MS fellowships, experience-conditional FNB entry and post-superspecialty FNBs. Do not plan Liver Transplant FNB as direct entry from a fresh MS.
- The initial baseline lists 2,677 seats across 22 listed awards; one further eligible award has no matching matrix entry. These seats are not reserved for General Surgery alone and are not current vacancies.
- Do not equate a corporate brand with hands-on training, or rural/non-metro location with guaranteed surgical volume, safety or higher disposable income.

Sources: [Competency-based postgraduate training programme for MS in General Surgery](https://www.nmc.org.in/storage/new/MS-Surgery.pdf) (PDF pp1-2 learning objectives; competencies, operative skills and assessment sections); [FET 2025 information bulletin: General Surgery fellowship feeders](https://www.nbe.edu.in/IB/FET%202025%20information%20bulletin%2012.01.2026_v2.pdf) (PDF pp25,30-33,130-132; Annexure B, Indian-candidate table); [CMC Vellore 2026-27 prospectus: General Surgery focused fellowships](https://www.cmch-vellore.edu/wp-content/uploads/sites/14/2026/06/course-prospectus-final-01-06-26.pdf) (PDF pp33-36 eligibility/duration; pp42,64-67 programme descriptions and hands-on training); [SGPGIMS 2026 brochure: surgery PDCC and SRHS routes](https://sgpgims.org.in/Home/er/2026/12.03.2026_Information%20Brochure%20for%20PDCC_PDAF_SRHS%20etc%202026.pdf) (PDF pp4,6-10,12,16; PDCC qualifications, SRHS department rows, duration and pay); [Consultant General Surgeon advert, Satna, Madhya Pradesh (PlacementIndia 1462771)](https://www.placementindia.com/job-detail/general-surgeon-in-pace-consultants-at-satna-1462771.htm) (Job title, annual salary header, experience/education fields, Job Description and interview location where applicable); [Laparoscopic General Surgeon advert, Mumbai metro; interview address Mumbra, Thane, Maharashtra (PlacementIndia 1273964)](https://www.placementindia.com/job-detail/laparoscopic-surgeon-in-meddco-at-mumbai-1273964.htm) (Job title, annual salary header, experience/education fields, Job Description and interview location where applicable); [AIIMS Raebareli ad-hoc Senior Resident recruitment, AIIMS/RBL/REC/SR/2026/532](https://aiimsrbl.edu.in/public/recruitments%5Cdownload%5C124) (pp1-2 departments and vacancies; p3 qualification, pay and appointment conditions; p4 excluded regular-service benefits)

**Open evidence gaps**

- No representative General Surgery salary survey by city, experience, sector and actual realised pay was established.
- No verified named corporate-chain salary grid or matched pre/post-fellowship earnings comparison was found. Unnamed recruiter clients are kept Private.
- Private adverts retain contradictions and retrieval dates. Relative 'posted days ago' text is not promoted to an exact publication date; other copies provide none.
- An advertised Urology role requiring MCh is a post-SS example, not a General Surgery salary or evidence of a universal MCh premium; other SS destination salaries remain unestablished.
- The selected initial NEET-SS 2025 matrices omit an eligible Paediatric Cardiothoracic Vascular Surgery MCh entry. Its national intake is not assumed zero. Later changes and INI-SS are excluded.
- No FET 2025 counselling seat totals have been reconstructed here; the fellowship list is an evidenced selection, not a national exhaustive register or list of open seats.
- Independent privileges, current faculty recognition, exact qualification equivalence, training bonds and overseas licensing require programme/employer/regulator checks.

### Editorial career planning

My shortlisting opinion: choose General Surgery if you want operative work, perioperative responsibility and the ability to develop a broad practice or later surgical focus. Its many SS options are valuable flexibility, but they do not compensate for weak hands-on MS/DNB training. I would place supervised primary-operator experience, judgement, complication rescue and recent graduate outcomes ahead of central/state/deemed/private ownership or a robot on a prospectus.

- **After MS/DNB:** Compare supported SR or surgical roles against your actual operating readiness. Government SR notices give INR 67,700 monthly basic plus applicable allowances. Do not benchmark a fresh MS against a consultant advert that expects independent laparoscopy.
- **FNB / institutional focused training:** Review direct post-MS options separately from experience-conditional Bariatric/Renal Transplant FNB and later post-SS fellowships. Check logbook, operating allocation, fees/stipend, recognition, bond and delayed earnings.
- **MCh/DrNB/DM training:** The selected NEET-SS 2025 baseline verifies 14 subjects and 23 award options, with 2,677 listed seats. One eligible award is not listed. Surgical Group, ENT Group and Medical Group apply to different destinations; INI-SS and direct six-year entry are separate.
- **Established general surgeon / post-SS surgeon:** A parent-specialty practice and a focused surgical career can both be viable, depending on competence and local funded work. Compare real written offers and case access. A Urology MCh advert is not an MS-only salary or evidence of what every other MCh earns.
- **Academic or service leadership:** Government faculty scales and contracts follow rank-specific eligibility and experience. Private Professor adverts with conflicting qualifications require verification; a title or years field alone does not establish faculty eligibility.

- Independent competence supported by training, registration and employer privileges; the ability to select cases safely matters as much as procedure counts.
- Theatre access, anaesthesia/ICU support, patient/referral flow, fair case allocation and continuity of follow-up.
- The chosen employment model: fixed salary, retainer, per-case work or ownership have different income stability, costs and liabilities.
- A focused fellowship/MCh with real supervised skills and funded demand; no automatic qualification-based income premium is established.
- Ask current trainees for primary-operator logs and follow graduates from several recent batches: first supported job, independence, city moves and fixed pay.
- Check who handles bleeding, conversion, bowel injury, postoperative sepsis and night emergencies; examine the backup available to a new surgeon.
- Obtain written salary, procedure incentives, duty roster, indemnity, case allocation, leave and restrictive clauses; hospital billings are not personal income.
- Validate exact fellowship entry requirements and recognised centre experience before budgeting for a route; general surgical experience alone does not qualify for every transplant FNB.
- No representative saturation measure, verified named-chain salary grid or matched fellowship/MCh pay uplift is established here. Recheck actual local offers before committing to high training costs.

## Training stipend policy

NBEMS prescribed basic training stipend before higher applicable state/central comparison

- Post-MD/MS/DNB DrNB Super Specialty: year 1: INR 49,000/month, year 2: INR 51,000/month, year 3: INR 53,000/month
- FNB: year 1: INR 51,000/month, year 2: INR 53,000/month
- Post-MBBS DNB Broad Specialty (comparison only): year 1: INR 43,000/month, year 2: INR 45,000/month, year 3: INR 47,000/month

- Private/state government hospitals: pay the NBEMS basic floor or applicable state government basic stipend, whichever is higher.
- Where the state pays DM/MCh residents a consolidated amount, that consolidated sum is the relevant comparison for DrNB/FNB.
- Where the state uses basic plus allowances under 7th CPC, the DrNB/FNB comparison is Pay Level 11, cells 1/2/3. Allowances are excluded from this NBEMS basic comparison, though a hospital may pay more.
- Central/railway/ESIC/PSU/autonomous institutions: use the higher applicable amount among NBEMS basic, the institution's non-academic senior resident basic, or Pay Level 11 cells 1/2/3; equivalent IDA basic where applicable.
- Institutions must revise rates as applicable state/central stipends change. The bulletin permits payment above its prescribed amounts.
- The hospital must not reduce stipend in lieu of accommodation (clause 5.16).

## Decision planning

### Use earnings evidence at the right career stage

- Immediately after MD/MS: compare supervised senior-resident and junior-consultant posts with actual duty hours, on-call nights and the skills you still need. Fresh qualification does not guarantee independent procedural readiness or a faculty appointment.
- During fellowship or DM/MCh/DrNB: calculate living costs, tuition, stipend, examination costs, any bond and foregone salary. A three-year training period can be financially worthwhile without increasing income immediately on graduation.
- First focused or superspecialist post: ask whether the advertised role actually uses your new skill, how much independent work you receive and what backup exists. A certificate alone does not demonstrate employability or grant universal privileges.
- Established consultant: separate fixed salary, variable incentives, professional fees and business profits. Department leadership and an ethical referral network can matter more than another short certificate. No uniform year-by-year salary ladder is established by these sources.
- Government careers: distinguish regular cadre posts, tenure senior residency, contract faculty and project appointments. Entry qualifications, experience, recruitment rules and benefits differ; a pay-band ceiling is not your starting basic pay.
- Industry and research careers: compare the actual role and required skills, such as medical affairs, pharmacovigilance, trials, diagnostics or research. A job titled pharmacologist or biochemist may accept non-medical graduates; it is not automatically an MD salary comparator.

### Tier 1 versus Tier 2: compare the whole offer

- Do not assume a metro always pays more. A smaller-city offer may compensate for relocation, language needs, emergency cover or lack of specialist backup. This research sample cannot estimate a causal city premium.
- Use two or three named cities you would realistically live in. For each branch, collect at least three current written offers with the same qualification, experience, duty hours and fixed-versus-variable pay definition before deciding on a likely earnings range.
- For Tier 1, investigate whether the hospital already has established consultants, who controls referrals, whether new consultants receive cases and whether a subspecialist can build a sustainable niche.
- For Tier 2, verify local paying demand, referral catchment, case mix, equipment downtime, ICU and anaesthesia support, competing services and the ability to refer complex cases safely. Unmet health need alone does not prove a funded job.
- Compare monthly fixed pay after known deductions with rent, commute, childcare, night duties, professional indemnity and relocation costs. Do not estimate take-home from basic pay without the employer's allowance and deduction breakdown.

### A practical saturation audit

- Treat saturation as a branch-by-city-by-role question. Competition for an academic post, a salaried corporate post, surgical case access and a new clinic are different markets.
- Ask recent graduates from at least three institutions where they actually joined, how long the search took, whether the post uses their training and what the fixed written offer was. Include ordinary graduates, not only star alumni.
- Check referral concentration and training quality: an oversupplied generalist market may still reward a demonstrable skill gap, but another fellowship has costs and no guaranteed premium.
- Compare job turnover and repeated adverts with funded filled posts. A long list of portal adverts can contain duplicates, old roles and unnamed clients; it is not a workforce shortage estimate.
- National specialist unemployment, matched salary distributions and an all-city saturation index were not established here. Interview warnings remain attributed perspectives in the video and Conclusions pages.

### Questions to ask a private hospital or corporate chain

- What is the guaranteed fixed monthly amount? Is the figure gross salary, annual CTC, professional retainer or expected total including incentives? Ask for the exact written breakup.
- What counts toward incentives, who owns collections, when are they paid and can the employer change the formula? Ask about refunds, revenue thresholds, exclusivity, notice period and restrictive clauses.
- How many consultants share the cases and on-call roster? Ask for actual recent OPD, admission and procedure volumes and the allocation method, with patient confidentiality protected.
- For procedures and diagnostics, who pays for equipment, consumables, staff, reporting software and indemnity? Confirm the clinical privileges and supervision attached to the post.
- For a fellowship, request a written rotation plan, supervised and independent case targets, faculty availability, assessment method, fees, stipend and recent alumni destinations. Marketing language is not a placement guarantee.

Locator: PDF page(s) 12. Evidence: The overall goal is to provide Indian Medical Graduates (IMG) a lifelong career path as family medicine specialists capable of providing comprehensive healthcare across varied settings from primary to secondary level. The DNB family medicine program intends to prepare a large pool of competent multiskilled doctors as Family Medicine Specialists. They are capable of practicing and functioning within the healthcare delivery system, fulfilling the critical human resource gap that exists across India within the community setting. The availability of competent and skilled family medicine experts and their integration with the healthcare delivery system shall improve communities' health status in urban, rural, and remote/ isolated areas. 1. DNB FAMILY MEDICINE OBJECTIVES: Educate and train family medicine specialist with following capabilities  Limits: Official competency/curriculum source, undated in the consulted copy; not evidence of current vacancies, income, local demand or automatic academic appointment eligibility.
